# Population Resilience Assessment

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Date** | 2026-04-04 |
| **Content kind** | mixed |
| **Sources** | articles-field-reports-2026-03-17.md, articles-field-reports-2026-03-24.md, articles-field-reports-2026-03-29.md, articles-homefront-2026-04-02.md, articles-homefront-2026-04-03.md, articles-homefront-2026-04-04.md, north 1-4.xlsx, north 2-4.xlsx, north 3-4.xlsx |
| **Articles analyzed** | 556 |

---

## Executive Summary

Across all eight components, the dominant behavioral pattern is one of sustained emergency routine rather than acute crisis response. Field reports from trained resilience professionals visiting northern border communities — Arab villages, small kibbutzim, elderly populations, and special-needs individuals — document a population that has largely adapted to prolonged wartime conditions: residents remain in their homes, follow HFC directives when sirens sound, maintain WhatsApp-based information networks, and draw on local volunteer structures and welfare departments for support. This pattern holds across ethnically and geographically diverse communities, from Druze villages on the Golan to Jewish border settlements in Ma'ale Yosef, with welfare departments conducting daily home visits, community coordinators managing decompression activities, and the majority of residents reporting compliance with protective guidelines. At the same time, a persistent structural split runs through multiple components: the protective and social infrastructure that functions adequately for the mobile, digitally connected, and Hebrew/Arabic-speaking majority fails systematically for several specific populations — bedridden elderly without home safe rooms, 600,000 Haredi residents using kosher phones that cannot receive HFC alerts, autistic children whose routines have collapsed, residents of settlements with zero warning time, and workers in agriculture or construction who cannot shelter. These failures are not incidental but structural, rooted in physical protection gaps, language and technology barriers, and the absence of dedicated coordinators for elderly and disabled populations in numerous communities.

The Passover holiday period introduced a second layer of behavioral complexity. Large-scale shelter compliance during Seder night rocket barrages — hundreds of thousands of families entering protected spaces mid-meal — demonstrates that the population can execute protective behaviors under pressure. However, the same period also reveals credibility erosion: IDF statements that Hezbollah disarmament is not a current war objective contradicted months of official commitments, provoking expressions of broken trust from northern municipal leaders and community coordinators who explicitly named the gap between the narrative they had been asked to maintain and the military reality they faced. This trust deficit does not appear to have produced behavioral non-compliance in the short term, but field reports from several northern settlements document that resilience is explicitly contingent on continued military progress — a conditional rather than unconditional coping posture that makes the population's sustained compliance dependent on an external variable.

---

## Components

**What each column means**

- **Assessment reliability** — How much we trust the **component score** (low / medium / high), based on both how strong the weighted evidence is and how broadly it appears across articles.
- **Evidence level** — A **0–100% certainty** score: how much *weighted* behavioral evidence we found for this component. **It is not “how good resilience is.”** High % means we had enough signal strength to be confident in the scoring step; the **Component score** (1–10) tells you favorable vs unfavorable.
- **Evidence base** — Number of behavioral signals tagged to this component.
- **Article coverage** — How many *distinct* articles in today’s sample contributed at least one signal, as a fraction of all articles analyzed.

**How Evidence level % is calculated (technical)** — Each signal adds `|mapping weight| × scope weight × reliability weight`. Those add up to *evidence mass*. Certainty = `1 − e^(−evidence_mass / 2)`, shown as %. It saturates toward 100% as mass grows. **Direction** (supporting vs opposing) is separate and shown under Evidence direction.

| # | Component | עברית | Assessment reliability | Evidence level | Evidence base | Article coverage |
|---|-----------|-------|------------------------|----------------|---------------|------------------|
| 1 | Narrative | נרטיב | high | 100% | 184 signals | 65/556 (12%) |
| 2 | Information, Communication, and Sharing | מידע, תקשורת ושיתוף | high | 100% | 159 signals | 75/556 (13%) |
| 3 | Effective Life-Saving Behavior | התנהגות אפקטיבית להצלת חיים | high | 100% | 306 signals | 83/556 (15%) |
| 4 | Functional Continuity | רציפות תפקודית | high | 100% | 252 signals | 86/556 (15%) |

## Detailed Analysis

### 📖 Narrative
*נרטיב*

**Assessment reliability:** high *(based on how much evidence was found and how broadly it appears across the sample)* | **Evidence level:** 100% *(certainty % from weighted evidence mass—not the same as “good” or “bad” resilience; use Component score for direction)*
**Evidence base:** 184 behavioral signals found in 65 of 556 articles *(11.7% of today's sample, low spread across sources)* | **Evidence direction:** more supporting than opposing *(supporting: 86.2, opposing: 3.1)*

The dominant coping narrative is circulating broadly and holds across Jewish and Arab communities of varying size, geography, and security exposure — field reports from over 60 northern localities document populations describing themselves as managing within emergency routine, adhering to guidelines, and maintaining community cohesion. However, two contradictory narrative strands coexist with this dominant frame. The first is a conditional resilience posture: residents in multiple border communities explicitly state that their willingness to remain is contingent on military progress, and the IDF's disclosure that Hezbollah disarmament is not a current war objective directly undermined the narrative that community coordinators and municipal leaders had been asked to sustain, producing documented expressions of betrayal in Braram, Abdon, Liman, and the Mateh Asher regional council. The second is a fatigue narrative concentrated in communities with longer exposure: Kiryat Shmona, Margelit, and several Arab towns report 'worn resilience' — life continues but under persistent psychological attrition. No evidence was found of the official narrative being broadly rejected in behavioral terms, but trust in political and military leadership is explicitly reported as damaged in a notable subset of northern communities.

- Field reports (Bre'em, Haram, Galil Elyon clusters): The dominant narrative across the majority of visited communities is one of maintained presence — residents in Netu'a state 'resilience exists as long as IDF does its job'; Kibbutz Dan and Mishgav Am residents report strong community functioning; Ghajar residents describe having survived previous rounds and express intention to remain.
- Field reports document a minority counter-narrative: leadership in Braram reports 'they told an unrealistic story, promised a quiet and safe border — it did not happen'; residents in Abdon state 'the army does not tell the truth'; Liman and Adamit residents report loss of trust toward military and political leadership.
- Northern municipal leaders (Avihai Stern, David Azulay, Assaf Langalben) responding to IDF statement that Hezbollah disarmament is not a war objective: 'Northern residents abandoned by State of Israel. Decision to back down from goal of disarming Hezbollah guarantees one thing: the next round is around the corner.' ([source](http://news.walla.co.il/item/3828240))
- Head of Mateh Asher Regional Council Moshe Davidovich: 'If the statements made in the army's briefing are accurate, we see this shift as a resounding slap in the face to residents who have remained in their homes since the start of the war, effectively serving as a human shield for Israel.' ([source](https://www.haaretz.com/israel-news/israel-security/2026-04-03/ty-article-live/cnn-iran-retains-half-of-missile-launchers-and-massive-drone-fleet/0000019d-5114-db3c-a3df-d995e3f30000?liveBlogItemId=1472985228))
- Petah Tikva resident: 'It's just amazing. 20 minutes after our neighborhood was in fear due to intense explosions and damage to our homes, within seconds everything flips and everyone returns life to routine as if nothing happened.' ([source](https://www.maariv.co.il/news/israel/article-1304454))
- Field reports document fatigue-adjusted coping in numerous communities: Kfar Kama residents report 'exhaustion from the prolonged situation, desire to return to routine'; Kiryat Shmona field report notes 'erosion among residents — the feeling oscillates between breakdown and hope'; Margelit reports 'if not given sense of security, in 10-20 years no residents — already barely managing to open kindergarten due to lack of children.'
- Over 4 weeks into Operation Roaring Lion, Israelis described as adapting, supporting one another, and proving resilience is its own form of victory; life settled into tense but determined rhythm. ([source](https://www.jpost.com/health-and-wellness/mind-and-spirit/article-891787))

### 📡 Information, Communication, and Sharing
*מידע, תקשורת ושיתוף*

**Assessment reliability:** high *(based on how much evidence was found and how broadly it appears across the sample)* | **Evidence level:** 100% *(certainty % from weighted evidence mass—not the same as “good” or “bad” resilience; use Component score for direction)*
**Evidence base:** 159 behavioral signals found in 75 of 556 articles *(13.5% of today's sample, low spread across sources)* | **Evidence direction:** more supporting than opposing *(supporting: 83.7, opposing: 2.6)*

Information distribution infrastructure is functioning across a wide range of communities, with local authorities operating multi-channel dissemination adapted to language and population type — including Arabic-language WhatsApp groups, kosher content lines, mosque-based channels, and proactive spokesperson video campaigns. This channel presence is clearly linked to compliance: communities with robust, trusted local communication (Rama, Arabbe, Deir el-Asad) report near-universal guideline adherence, while communities with thinner infrastructure (Nachaf, Magar) show more fragmented uptake. However, two distinct effectiveness gaps are documented alongside this infrastructure strength. The first is a population coverage gap: 600,000 Haredi residents cannot receive HFC alerts via their phones, and language barriers exclude newly arrived immigrant populations in Tiberias and elsewhere from real-time municipal updates. The second is a strategic credibility gap: the IDF's disclosure that Hezbollah disarmament is not a current war objective contradicted repeated official commitments and produced an information environment in which community coordinators — who had been transmitting the official narrative — reported that their residents now questioned the truthfulness of all state communications. A siren failure in Kiryat Shmona with no advance warning further illustrates that guidance presence does not guarantee coverage in all scenarios.

- Field reports document multi-channel information distribution across the majority of visited communities: WhatsApp groups, Facebook pages, municipality websites, Telegram, loudspeaker announcements, and mosque-based channels are all in active use; Rama field report documents weekly illustrative videos of incident sites distributed by the municipal spokesperson to reinforce threat perception and guideline adherence.
- 600,000 Haredi residents use outdated kosher phones that fail to receive Home Front Command early warnings of missile attacks. ([source](https://www.haaretz.co.il/tmr/weekend/2026-04-03/ty-article-magazine/.premium/0000019d-441c-d9ad-adbf-7e9f17430000))
- Tiberias field report documents language gaps for new immigrants (French, Russian, Amharic, and Koki for Bnei Menashe) and a coverage gap in the Haredi content line (2,000 of 3,500 households not yet connected).
- IDF briefing contradicted previous public commitments by PM Netanyahu and Chief of Staff Zammir that Hezbollah disarmament was a war objective; briefing stated disarmament is not a current war goal, causing documented anger among northern residents. ([source](https://www.mako.co.il/news-military/2026_q2/Article-338f026fcf15d91026.htm))
- Rocket hit Kiryat Shmona without alert siren activation; residents unwarned of impact — IDF investigating. ([source](https://www.maariv.co.il/breaking-news/article-1304857))
- Google deployed visual map display directly in Android emergency alerts, showing threat zones in real-time via Cell Broadcast technology, eliminating need to unlock device or open external apps. ([source](https://www.srugim.co.il/1301261))
- Football team manager Chagai: league decision to require match in Tzfat is 'disconnected from reality, does not account for real fear of people'; team faces technical loss and heavy fine if they don't travel — illustrating operational guidance that does not account for real-world constraints. ([source](https://www.one.co.il/Article/518668.html))
- Ramat Gan mayor Carmel Shama Hacohen: 'They always act according to the guidelines and that saved them. They didn't hesitate and despite the late hour and cold outside, they left their home and made it to the shelter. Following the guidelines saves lives.' ([source](https://www.maariv.co.il/news/israel/article-1304657))

### 🛡️ Effective Life-Saving Behavior
*התנהגות אפקטיבית להצלת חיים*

**Assessment reliability:** high *(based on how much evidence was found and how broadly it appears across the sample)* | **Evidence level:** 100% *(certainty % from weighted evidence mass—not the same as “good” or “bad” resilience; use Component score for direction)*
**Evidence base:** 306 behavioral signals found in 83 of 556 articles *(14.9% of today's sample, low spread across sources)* | **Evidence direction:** more supporting than opposing *(supporting: 124.8, opposing: 6.9)*

Compliance with HFC protective guidelines is the dominant behavioral pattern across the population, evidenced most clearly by the Seder night response — hundreds of thousands of families interrupting holiday meals to enter shelters — and confirmed by field reports from the large majority of visited communities. The perception of genuine life-threat is widespread and has been reinforced by direct impact events: field reports from Rama, Ghajar, and Kiryat Ata all document that local rocket or missile impacts produced increased compliance in the days immediately following. However, a structural split separates compliant from non-compliant sub-populations: complacency is documented in communities experiencing fewer recent alerts, where residents are returning to playgrounds, continuing travel during sirens, and patronizing businesses without protected spaces. A second, distinct failure mode involves populations who cannot comply regardless of intent — residents with zero warning time in communities like Kiryat Shmona and Majdal Shams, Haredi residents without alert-capable phones, and bedridden elderly without accessible shelter. Field reports from Nachaf and Yanuch-Jatt document community response teams tripling their volunteer numbers and conducting outreach specifically to address the gap between guideline knowledge and implementation among populations facing physical or structural barriers.

- Hundreds of thousands of Israelis forced into shelters during Passover Seder meals on first day of holiday due to Hezbollah rocket barrage — roads emptied similar to Yom Kippur as residents sheltered. ([source](https://www.timesofisrael.com/hezbollah-fires-around-80-rockets-at-north-on-1st-day-of-passover-2-lightly-injured/))
- Couple from Ramat Gan: 'We follow the guidelines and when we received the alert, we left in the middle of the night, got in the car and drove to the shelter. When we were in the shelter we heard a strong explosion, but we didn't know there was a hit in our house. Only when we returned did we see the extensive destruction.' ([source](https://www.maariv.co.il/news/israel/article-1304657))
- Field reports document partial compliance in several communities: Boukea'ta field report states 'residents know the guidelines well but only approximately 70% follow them'; Majdal Shams report notes residents 'aware of guidelines but execute partially'; Sachnin report states 'a non-negligible portion of the population does not follow guidelines and complacency exists.'
- Director of Religious Services Ministry sent stern letter to Rabbi of the Western Wall regarding Rashbi site in Meron operating in full routine contrary to HFC guidelines; site open without HFC approval; ministry demanded immediate compliance or closure. ([source](http://news.walla.co.il/item/3828221))
- Field reports from multiple communities note increasing complacency linked to reduced alert frequency: Lower Galilee report documents children returning to playgrounds and a 'kind of indifference because there is time until the alarm'; Kfar Kama notes 'fear index declining over time, increasing complacency.'
- Kiryat Shmona field report: residents 'believe guidelines can help but the reality here is impossible because warning time almost does not exist.'
- Many businesses resuming operations without waiting for war's end; 200 people sitting in restaurant without protected space; businesses understand compensation is meager and prefer to take risks rather than follow guidelines. ([source](https://www.haaretz.co.il/tmr/weekend/2026-04-03/ty-article-magazine/.premium/0000019d-43da-d068-a19f-4fda9ba80000))
- 600,000 Haredi residents use outdated kosher phones that fail to receive HFC early warnings of missile attacks — structural non-receipt rather than volitional non-compliance. ([source](https://www.haaretz.co.il/tmr/weekend/2026-04-03/ty-article-magazine/.premium/0000019d-441c-d9ad-adbf-7e9f17430000))

### ⚙️ Functional Continuity
*רציפות תפקודית*

**Assessment reliability:** high *(based on how much evidence was found and how broadly it appears across the sample)* | **Evidence level:** 100% *(certainty % from weighted evidence mass—not the same as “good” or “bad” resilience; use Component score for direction)*
**Evidence base:** 252 behavioral signals found in 86 of 556 articles *(15.5% of today's sample, low spread across sources)* | **Evidence direction:** more supporting than opposing *(supporting: 41, opposing: 35.9)*

Functional continuity is bifurcated between essential services and social-educational infrastructure. Essential services — supermarkets, clinics, municipal offices, and critical energy infrastructure — are operating, with notable improvements including Leviathan gas platform resumption and expanded airport passenger quotas. Social and educational infrastructure, by contrast, is substantially disrupted: formal schooling has been closed for an extended period across the conflict-affected north, the Passover holiday has compounded this with an additional two-week break, and informal education operates in severely constrained shelter environments with reduced instructional staff. Field reports identify the absence of educational frameworks as the dominant functional continuity failure, with parents unable to maintain work schedules, adolescents without structured activity roaming streets and roadways, and elderly populations isolated in homes without access to social or day-care facilities. The extension of reserve duty from 6 to 9 weeks imposes a second tier of disruption — employers warn of economic consequences, and families with reservists mobilized face compounded childcare and income loss. The differential between communities that have opened informal educational programs in shelters (Masade, Beit Hillel, Shlomi) and those that have not is the most actionable functional continuity split in today's evidence.

- Field reports across the northern border community cluster document a consistent pattern: commercial and essential services (supermarkets, clinics, pharmacies) remain open; formal education is either closed (conflict zone) or on Passover break; informal education and decompression activities are operating at reduced capacity in shelters; parents report difficulty maintaining work schedules without childcare.
- Field reports from Braram and multiple Ma'ale Yosef settlements document continuous shelter residence due to absence of home safe rooms — public shelters not designed for extended occupancy, limiting all social and educational activity.
- IDF announced reserve service extended from 6 weeks to 9 weeks for 2026 due to ongoing war; employers warn of business impact and reduced motivation to employ reservists. ([source](https://www.themarker.com/career/2026-04-04/ty-article/.premium/0000019d-5491-df19-a9bf-fc97866d0000))
- Energy Ministry announced resumption of Leviathan gas platform operations after approximately 5-week shutdown; Karish remains closed; Tamar continues normal operation. ([source](https://www.calcalist.co.il/local_news/article/rkt11qq2owl))
- Transportation Ministry eased flight restrictions at Ben Gurion Airport: passenger quota increased from 50 to 80, plus humanitarian cases, effective Sunday. ([source](https://www.timesofisrael.com/liveblog_entry/starting-sunday-israel-will-allow-up-to-90-passengers-on-flights-leaving-ben-gurion-airport/))
- Ministry of Health expanded medical response in Kiryat Shmona, operating 24/7 emergency and trauma care with specialist orthopedic/emergency physician, X-ray services, operating room, wound treatment, infection prevention, casting, and psychological support. ([source](https://www.kikar.co.il/israel-news/tcszrg))
- Church of the Holy Sepulcher closed to public during Holy Week due to Iran war; limited prayer framework approved for clergy and staff only. ([source](https://www.timesofisrael.com/latin-patriarch-holds-limited-prayers-at-holy-sepulchre-after-palm-sunday-uproar/))
- War added approximately 15,000 wounded to rehabilitation department; most apartments in Israel not adapted even for basic accessibility; wounded soldiers struggling to find accessible housing. ([source](https://www.mako.co.il/finances-real-estate/Article-0f0aa38918f3d91027.htm))

### 🤝 Community Capital and Resources
*הון ומשאבי קהילה*

**Assessment reliability:** high *(based on how much evidence was found and how broadly it appears across the sample)* | **Evidence level:** 100% *(certainty % from weighted evidence mass—not the same as “good” or “bad” resilience; use Component score for direction)*
**Evidence base:** 463 behavioral signals found in 67 of 556 articles *(12.1% of today's sample, low spread across sources)* | **Evidence direction:** more supporting than opposing *(supporting: 151.5, opposing: 17.2)*

Community capital resources are active and coordinated across the majority of visited communities, with welfare departments conducting proactive daily outreach to vulnerable populations, volunteer units responding to real-time incidents, and informal mutual aid networks providing food, childcare, and emotional support at the neighborhood level. The split within this component is between communities with institutionalized volunteer coordination — formal SAR units, designated volunteer coordinators, trained response plans — and those where volunteer infrastructure has eroded or was never built: Yavneel, Ma'alot-Tarshiha, and several Mevuot Hermon settlements report that previous volunteer structures were dismantled for budgetary reasons and have not been reconstituted, leaving communities dependent on a small pool of informal helpers with no coordination mechanism. A second structural gap is the depletion of the volunteer pool by reserve call-ups and economic necessity — documented in Magar, where student volunteers and many trained responders have been absorbed into military or employment obligations. Cross-sector cooperation between municipalities, HFC volunteer corps, welfare departments, and community organizations is functioning where structures pre-existed but is absent where they were not previously established.

- Field reports document active volunteer structures in the large majority of visited communities: SAR units, Magen David Adom volunteers, national service members, and community social workers are operating in coordinated frameworks across Druze, Muslim Arab, Christian Arab, and Jewish communities.
- Field reports document specific volunteer gaps linked to reserve call-ups: Magar field report notes 'difficulty recruiting volunteers because many who volunteered were called up under Order 8, some are at open workplaces, students who are a large volunteer force are in remote learning' — a structural depletion of the volunteer pool.
- Kibbutz residents hosted 35 residents from northern settlements (Metula, Dan, Snir, Manara, Beit Hillel) for Seder night and three days, providing full board, meals, activities for children, and gifts. ([source](https://www.maariv.co.il/news/israel/article-1304397))
- National Insurance Institute accelerated unemployment benefit payments to April instead of May; approved approximately 30,000 applications from approximately 50,000 new requests. ([source](https://www.srugim.co.il/1301236-ישר-לחשבון-ביטוח-לאומי-בשורה-אדירה))
- Online fundraiser for flower shop with shattered windows raised NIS 45,000 (~$14,000) to cover repair costs; community members urged to purchase bouquets or donate to sustain 17-year-old business. ([source](https://www.timesofisrael.com/israelis-spend-passover-under-shadow-of-iran-war-as-businesses-cling-to-survival/))
- Field report from Yavneel documents volunteer corps requiring full rebuilding — currently 15 volunteers, no forward movement on expanding the pool; field reports from Ma'alot-Tarshiha and Mevuot Hermon document similar capacity gaps, with volunteer coordination roles previously eliminated for budget reasons not yet restored.
- Clalit data: 120% increase in psychological support center visits since war began. ([source](https://www.bokra.net/Article-1591424))
- Shlomi Mayor Gabi Naaman and local council organized community Seder, inviting residents from neighboring hard-hit communities to participate. ([source](https://www.jpost.com/israel-news/article-891959))

### 👤 Leadership
*מנהיגות*

**Assessment reliability:** high *(based on how much evidence was found and how broadly it appears across the sample)* | **Evidence level:** 99% *(certainty % from weighted evidence mass—not the same as “good” or “bad” resilience; use Component score for direction)*
**Evidence base:** 23 behavioral signals found in 20 of 556 articles *(3.6% of today's sample, very low spread across sources)* | **Evidence direction:** more supporting than opposing *(supporting: 7.5, opposing: 1)*

Local leadership is functioning at a generally adequate level across the majority of visited communities, with community coordinators, HFC volunteer corps commanders, and welfare department heads actively present and described as a source of stability by field professionals. The more significant leadership deficit is at the national and military level: the IDF's disclosure that Hezbollah disarmament is not a current war objective directly contradicted months of official public commitments, eroding the credibility of the narrative that local leaders had been asked to sustain and producing documented distrust in multiple northern border communities. A separate coordination failure was documented in Tel Aviv, where police used the HFC as a false justification for dispersing a political protest — an incident that further weakened institutional credibility. The siren failure in Kiryat Shmona and the police misrepresentation episode are analytically distinct: one reflects an operational failure in protective systems, the other a deliberate misuse of the security apparatus's authority. No evidence was found of local community leadership being perceived as absent or incompetent in the majority of communities, though several settlements in the Ma'ale Yosef cluster document community coordinators operating under conditions of deep community erosion with limited external support.

- Field reports from Mattat, Horfish, Batzet, and Ghajar document local leadership functioning effectively, with community coordinators at full capacity, HFC volunteer corps active, and strong cooperation among role-holders.
- Field reports from Braram and Aven Menachem document loss of trust in political and military leadership: 'they told an unrealistic story, promised a quiet and safe border — it did not happen'; 'deep distrust of authority'; 'the army does not tell the truth.'
- IDF clarified that Hezbollah disarmament is not included in current war objectives despite repeated public commitments by PM Netanyahu, Defense Minister, and Chief of Staff that 'we will not stop until it is disarmed.' ([source](https://www.mako.co.il/news-military/2026_q2/Article-338f026fcf15d91026.htm))
- Chief of Staff Eyal Zamir conducted Passover Seder with 146th brigade at front lines during wartime — visible leadership presence during the holiday period. ([source](https://www.israelnationalnews.com/news/424968))
- Defense Minister Israel Katz held situation assessment with Chief of Staff, intelligence heads, and senior security officials; issued direct threat to Hezbollah leader Qassem. ([source](https://www.kipa.co.il/%D7%97%D7%93%D7%A9%D7%95%D7%AA/1222146-0/))
- Tel Aviv police decision to disperse Saturday anti-government protest was made by junior officer under district pressure, not senior HFC officials; officers falsely announced dispersal was due to 'situation assessment' with HFC when no formal assessment occurred — a documented coordination failure and misleading public communication. ([source](https://www.haaretz.co.il/news/politics/war-2023/2026-04-03/ty-article-live/0000019d-5030-d220-a9bf-73f531200000?liveBlogItemId=1400245265))
- Supreme Court President Isaac Amit criticized police position on protest restrictions: 'Political protest freedom exists even during war — and requires enhanced protection.' ([source](http://news.walla.co.il/item/3828288))
- Rocket hit Kiryat Shmona without alert siren activation; IDF investigating why alert was not activated — a leadership accountability issue in operational terms. ([source](https://news.walla.co.il/break/3828339))

### 🔗 Belonging and Solidarity
*שייכות וסולידריות*

**Assessment reliability:** high *(based on how much evidence was found and how broadly it appears across the sample)* | **Evidence level:** 100% *(certainty % from weighted evidence mass—not the same as “good” or “bad” resilience; use Component score for direction)*
**Evidence base:** 272 behavioral signals found in 59 of 556 articles *(10.6% of today's sample, low spread across sources)* | **Evidence direction:** more supporting than opposing *(supporting: 124, opposing: 2.8)*

Belonging and solidarity at the interpersonal and community level are functioning robustly across the majority of visited communities, with field reports consistently documenting neighbor-to-neighbor assistance during alerts, organized hosting of displaced northern residents for Passover, food distribution to economically vulnerable families, and spontaneous grassroots mutual aid. The dominant behavioral pattern is one of communities drawing tighter together under shared threat rather than fracturing along sectoral or ethnic lines — notably, this holds across Arab, Druze, and Jewish communities of diverse composition. Two splits within this component are analytically significant. First, intra-community solidarity is present in most communities but does not translate to inter-community solidarity in several mixed cities: Shfaram field reports explicitly note that each of the city's Christian, Muslim, and Jewish communities operates independently without coordinated cross-community initiatives. Second, solidarity is interpersonally rich but often lacks coordination infrastructure in communities where the volunteer coordinator role is unfilled — meaning that willingness to help exists but is not efficiently directed to those who most need it, particularly elderly and disabled residents without active family support networks.

- Field reports from the large majority of visited Arab and Jewish communities document high solidarity scores, with communities describing themselves as 'one family' (Sager, small Druze villages), 'no groups outside the camp' (Rama, Ghajar, Ein Kiniya), and reporting that neighbor-to-neighbor help activates immediately during alerts.
- Kibbutz residents hosted 35 residents from northern settlements (Metula, Dan, Snir, Manara, Beit Hillel) for Seder night and three days, providing full board, meals, activities for children, and gifts. ([source](https://www.maariv.co.il/news/israel/article-1304397))
- Kibbutz member Azulai: 'It's not just Seder night. We are hosting them from the day before the holiday until Saturday. We organized everything we could to give them a good feeling.' ([source](https://www.maariv.co.il/news/israel/article-1304397))
- Shlomi Mayor Gabi Naaman and local council organized community Seder, inviting residents from neighboring hard-hit communities to participate. ([source](https://www.jpost.com/israel-news/article-891959))
- Nachaf field report documents building superintendent opening shelters, WhatsApp groups filling with updates and assistance offers, and neighbors caring for elderly and families with small children during alerts.
- Mazor pharmacy in Jerusalem's German Colony accepted return of unopened medical supplies after customer's mother died; decided to use proceeds for charitable purposes rather than refund. ([source](https://www.jpost.com/israel-news/article-891789))
- Field reports from Shfaram note 'no cross-community solidarity initiatives between the communities — each community for itself,' representing a distinct pattern in a mixed-religion city where intra-community solidarity is present but inter-community solidarity is absent.
- Seven-year-old girl organized spontaneous Purim celebration in building lobby during wartime; approximately 20 residents including children and elderly participated. ([source](https://www.globes.co.il/news/article.aspx?did=1001538937))
- Field reports from Sachmin and Ma'alot-Tarshiha note that solidarity 'exists in small initiatives' but is not systematically organized or coordinated — present at the interpersonal level but not institutionalized.

---

### ❤️ Physical and Mental Wellbeing (At-Risk Populations)
*דאגה לרווחה הפיזית והנפשית בדגש על אוכלוסיות סיכון*

**Assessment reliability:** high *(based on how much evidence was found and how broadly it appears across the sample)* | **Evidence level:** 100% *(certainty % from weighted evidence mass—not the same as “good” or “bad” resilience; use Component score for direction)*
**Evidence base:** 621 behavioral signals found in 157 of 556 articles *(28.2% of today's sample, low spread across sources)* | **Evidence direction:** more supporting than opposing *(supporting: 143.6, opposing: 64.9)*

Wellbeing responses for at-risk populations are operating across most visited communities, with welfare departments conducting daily outreach, resilience centers open and receiving referrals, and psychological first aid available for panic casualties. The distribution of response quality, however, is uneven in ways that correspond to pre-existing institutional capacity. Communities with well-established welfare infrastructure — Arabbe, Rama, Kfar Yasif — report comprehensive daily contact with elderly, disabled, and at-risk populations, proactive food and equipment distribution, and active tracking of individuals who do not respond to calls. Communities with thin or depleted welfare capacity — several border settlements in Ma'ale Yosef, Kasra-Sumei, and Ma'alot-Tarshiha — document significant service gaps, compounded in some cases by welfare workers themselves being unable to attend due to school closures. Three specific at-risk population failures are informative rather than likely reporting gaps: the complete absence of any protected space for bedridden elderly in several northern settlements, documented by field professionals; the condition of autistic children whose behavioral deterioration is quantified in a systematic survey (55% worsening since the operation began); and the absence of multidisciplinary psychiatric care for residents with pre-existing serious mental illness, which the current resilience center model does not address. The suicide rate among soldiers — over 80 since October 2023 — represents an unmet need in a population largely absent from civilian-facing service reports.

- Field reports document welfare departments conducting daily proactive outreach to elderly, disabled, and nursing-dependent populations across the majority of visited communities: home visits, phone check-ins, food voucher distribution, and coordination with nursing care companies are all reported as operational.
- Magen David Adom: Since start of Operation Roaring Lion, 2,250 people treated — 1,757 physical injuries, 493 panic cases; 550 injured from missile impacts including 19 killed, 1 critical, 21 severe, 37 moderate, 472 mild. ([source](https://www.bokra.net/Article-1591609))
- Ministry of Health: 6,594 casualties evacuated to hospitals since Operation Roaring Lion began; currently 125 hospitalized including 2 critical, 14 severe, 24 moderate, 79 mild. ([source](https://www.maariv.co.il/breaking-news/article-1304575))
- Survey of 168 parents by autism association: 55% report worsening of children's condition since Operation Roaring Lion — manifested in bedwetting, rage attacks, violence, lack of treatments, anxiety, and clinging to parents. ([source](http://news.walla.co.il/item/3827964))
- Clalit data: 120% increase in psychological support center visits; elderly and others avoiding medical follow-up for chronic conditions due to fear of leaving homes; ignoring symptoms like chest pain and shortness of breath. ([source](https://www.bokra.net/Article-1591424))
- Field report from Mevuot Hermon documents 'severe shortage of multidisciplinary mental health teams including psychiatrists, psychologists, and social workers' for populations with pre-existing psychiatric conditions — the resilience center addresses acute war stress but not pre-existing serious mental illness.
- Field reports from Kfar Giladi and Margelit document bedridden elderly patients without ability to reach any protected space — described as 'major challenge' with no current solution.
- Field reports from Kasra-Sumei document welfare department operating with reduced staff because most female workers are mothers who are home with children due to school closures — creating a circular service gap.
- Psychotherapist reports clients experiencing 'continuous traumatic stress': children home from school, parents unable to work, businesses collapsing, families stretched with loved ones on reserve duty. ([source](https://www.jpost.com/health-and-wellness/mind-and-spirit/article-891787))
- Over 80 soldiers have committed suicide since Oct. 7; IDF veterans with PTSD meeting Knesset committees for months seeking resources for PTSD epidemic. ([source](https://www.jpost.com/health-and-wellness/mind-and-spirit/article-891738))

---

## Evidence Quality

The signal base combines a high proportion of direct observational field reports from resilience professionals (no URLs, primary evidence) with a substantial volume of cited news sources containing direct resident and official quotations, yielding an evidence base of relatively high behavioral specificity, though the field report layer is geographically concentrated in northern border communities and may not represent the full national distribution.

---

- "[ג'ש (גוש חלב)] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=100% (100%, 100%, 100%) — ביישוב קיים מערך גדול של מתנדבים בחירום המחולקים לצוותים , ישנה בחורה מטעם הרשות הממונה על מערך המתנדבים והמתכללת את פעילותו לאורך כל השנה . מתקיימים פעילויות של תמתנדבים יחידים כמו אומנים מתוך היישוב בתחום הפגה . קיימת תכנית פעולה בחירום לצוותי המתנדבים . בתקופה זו התקיימו מפגשים עם צוותי המתנדבים"
- "[דיר אל אסד] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=100% (100%, 100%, 100%) — ישנם מספר גופי מתנדבים ומאורגנים בצורה ממוסדת כמו : סע"ר , מתנדבי מד"א , סוות צח"ש ,עמותות ."
- "[דיר חנא] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=100% (100%, 100%, 100%) — מרגישים רוח התנדבות בקרב התושבים"
- "[חורפיש] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=100% (100%, 100%, 100%) — ללא שינוי"
- "[טבריה] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=50% (50%, 50%, 50%) — משרת רכזת המתנדבים אוישה לפני כחצי שנה, ואוחזת כעת בשלבי מיפוי כוחות התנדבים והצמדתם על בסיס החלוקה לרובעים, לפי כישורים ומיומנויות. בחודש שעבר בוצע אימון מתנדבים נוער ותרגול של פתיחת מתקני קליטה, חלוקת מים ותגבור גני ילדים וכד', ומתנדבי הנוער פעילים ותומכים ומסייעים לקשישים בהבאת תרופות, מזון וכד' וכן בפנוי, ניקיון וארגון של מקלטים משותפים ברחבי העיר עבור תושבים שאין בכוחם להכשיר א ת המקלט המשותף לדיירי הבניין.
לפני שבועיים, בוצע לראשונה תרגיל פתיחת והפעלת מס"ר בשילוב המתנדבים החלוצים ברשות, ואחרי חג הפסח צפוי להיות תרגיל נוסף שכזה, לאחר הפקת לקחים ותובנות שהועלו מהתרגיל הראשון."

צוות סכר בהקמה עדין מגיסיים עוד אנשים"

- "[כפר יאסיף] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=100% (100%, 100%, 100%) — בקהילה קיימים ארגונים ומתנדבים המסייעים בזמן חירום, כמו חלוקת ציוד, סיוע למשפחות ותמיכה באנשים הזקוקים לעזרה. פעילות זו מחזקת את הקהילה ומסייעת בהתמודדות עם מצבי חירום"
- "[כפר כמא] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=100% (100%, 100%, 100%) — לל"ש"
- "[כפר מנדא] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=75% (75%, 75%, 75%) — רשתות חברתיות, מתנדבים, ידע מקומי, ארגונים אזרחיים ותשתיות קהילתיות. למרות הפוטנציאל הגבוה, במצבי חירום מתגלים לעיתים אתגרים ופערים שמגבילים את היכולת למצות משאבים אלה באופן מיטבי. 
במהלך מצבי חירום נוצרים במהירות קבוצות סיוע ב־WhatsApp או Telegram, אך לעיתים אין גורם שמרכז את המידע ולכן קשה לדעת היכן יש צורך אמיתי."
- "[כרמיאל] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=100% (100%, 100%, 100%) — ללש"
- "[מגאר] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=67% (75%, 50%, 75%) — בעיר מע'אר , ישנם מתנדבים רבים ובמסגרות רבות , תנועות נוער, שירות לאומי אזרחי , עמותות כמו אקי"ם , מתנדבים של יחידת חילוץ , מתנדבים יעודיים ברווחה .
היום יש קושי לגייס מתנדבים כי הרבה מאלה שהתנדבו גוייסו בצוי 8 , חלק נמצא בעבודה במקומות העבודה הפתוחים , סטודנטים שהם כוח התנדבות גדול נמצאים בלימודים מרחוק ."
- "[מג'ד אל-כרום] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=75% (75%, 75%, 75%) — במצבי חירום קיים צורך משמעותי במיצוי משאבי הקהילה כדי לסייע לאוכלוסיות פגיעות, במיוחד קשישים ואנשים עם מוגבלויות. אחד האתגרים המרכזיים הוא מחסור בכוח אדם זמין שיכול לספק סיוע אישי ומהיר לאוכלוסיות אלו. לעיתים מספר המתנדבים או העובדים הסוציאליים אינו מספיק כדי לתת מענה לכל מי שזקוק לעזרה."

- "[מג'דל שמס] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=92% (100%, 75%, 100%) — הגישה של הרשות מחמירה לכן גם כשיש משאבים לא מפעילים אותם למשל הייתה המלצה לפתוח את מעדון הקשישים בתוך מרחב מוגן שקיים - הרשות התנגדה 
 מרכז חוסן פעיל מאד ומקיים פעילות הפגה באמצעות הזום"
- "[מסעדה] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=67% (75%, 50%, 75%) — אין פערים במיצוי משאבי קהילה"
- "[משגב] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=92% (100%, 75%, 100%) — נכנסה בעלת תפקיד חדשה לתחום ריכוז ההתנדבות אחרי תקופה מאד ארוכה שלא היה מאוייש. באופן כללי, יש התגייסות יפה של מתנדבים ביישובים. האתגר הוא לנהל את המשאב הזה נכון כדי למנוע שחיקה של האנשים לאורך זמן. בדגש עכשיו על כניסה לחופשת פסח ותגבור הפעילויות החינוכיות והבלתי פורמליות והתבססות הרבה מהפעמים על מתנדבים במקום או בנוסף לצוותים האורגניים."

- "[סאג'ור] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=75% (75%, 75%, 75%) — תמיד יש חשש מריבוי התקהלות במידה ויהיה אירוע וזה מה שקרה שנפלו שברי יירוטים בכפר"
- "[סח'נין] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=58% (50%, 50%, 75%) — על אף הנכונות הרבה באוכלוסיה לתרום אין גוף המאגד ומנצל את המשאב האנושי 
יש יוזמות פה ושם אבל זה רחוק מלהיות מנוצל כראוי

קיים צורך משמעותי בהתנדבות בתחומים של סיוע לאוכלוסיות מוחלשות (קשישים, משפחות במצוקה), תמיכה רגשית-חברתית במיוחד בתקופות חירום, סיוע לוגיסטי וחלוקת משאבים, חינוך והכוונה לבני נוער, והנגשת מידע וזכויות לתושבים. בנוסף, יש צורך בחיזוק מערך ההתנדבות לשעת חירום והכשרת פעילים"

יש רשימות .
אתגרים ופערים , הכשרה פרקטית וציוד ."
- "[צפת] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=75% (75%, 75%, 75%) — ניתן לציין לחיוב כי קיימת נכונות גבוהה מצד תושבים לקחת חלק בפעילות התנדבותית, המעידה על תחושת אחריות קהילתית וסולידריות עמוקה. תושבים רבים מביעים רצון לתרום, לסייע לאוכלוסיות פגיעות ולתמוך בפעילות העירונית. לדוגמא- טיפול בתקלות מקלוט, סיוע להורים שנמצאים עם ילדיהם בבית(השירות הפסיכולוגי) הפעלות בזום לילדים ועוד. פעילויות וחלוקת ערכות הפגה לילדים במקלטים. התנדבות בתחומי לימודים והעשרה. גרעין מתנדבים שמגיע לעזור לנשות המילואמניקים בביתם בניקיון ארוחות בייביסיטר תיקונים קטנים בבית וחבילות שי. כמו כן, נפתחה קבוצת וואצפ ייעודית לנשות המילואים על מנת להקל על העומס."
- "[קצרין] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=83% (75%, 100%, 75%) — אין אתגרים/פערים שידועים לי, יחד עם זאת המתנדבים פועלים במספר ערוצים-
1. עזרה למשפחות המילואים- ביביסיטר, ניקיון, בישול
2. ילדי צמי"ד- פעילויות הפגה בכל יום
3. חלוקת מזון לנזקקים
4.צח"שים- מגיעים לבתי תושבים בגיל השלישי להפגת בדידות
5. הכנת מארזים לוותיקים וחלוקה"

- "[כסרא סמיע] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=67% (75%, 75%, 50%) — ישנן מתנדבים בנות שירות לאומי אשר נמצאות בחמ״ל המועצה וחיילי יקל״ר ונותנים  מענה לתושבים"
- "[כפר כמא] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=100% (100%, 100%, 100%) — לל"ש"
- "[כפר מנדא] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=75% (75%, 75%, 75%) — רשתות חברתיות, מתנדבים, ידע מקומי, ארגונים אזרחיים ותשתיות קהילתיות. למרות הפוטנציאל הגבוה, במצבי חירום מתגלים לעיתים אתגרים ופערים שמגבילים את היכולת למצות משאבים אלה באופן מיטבי. 
במהלך מצבי חירום נוצרים במהירות קבוצות סיוע ב־WhatsApp או Telegram, אך לעיתים אין גורם שמרכז את המידע ולכן קשה לדעת היכן יש צורך אמיתי."
- "[כרמיאל] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=100% (100%, 100%, 100%) — ללש"
- "[מבואות חרמון] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=50% (50%, 50%, 50%) — רוב המתנדבים מנוהלים בסופו של דבר במסגרת היישוב. בישובים צמודי גדר יש כנראה איסור לעלות (של מכיניסטים למשל) מסיבות בטחוניות וכן בחלק מהישובים אין את המקלטים המתאימים לפעילות המתנדבים."
- "[מגאר] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=50% (50%, 50%, 50%) — בעיר מע'אר , ישנם מתנדבים רבים ובמסגרות רבות , תנועות נוער, שירות לאומי אזרחי , עמותות כמו אקי"ם , מתנדבים של יחידת חילוץ , מתנדבים יעודיים ברווחה .
היום יש קושי לגייס מתנדבים כי הרבה מאלה שהתנדבו גוייסו בצוי 8 , חלק נמצא בעבודה במקומות העבודה הפתוחים , סטודנטים שהם כוח התנדבות גדול נמצאים בלימודים מרחוק . הצארכות המלחמה משפיעה במידה רבה על אחזקת המתנדבים, ולכן היום גם פחות אנשים פונים להתנדב"
- "[מג'ד אל-כרום] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=75% (75%, 75%, 75%) — במצבי חירום קיים צורך משמעותי במיצוי משאבי הקהילה כדי לסייע לאוכלוסיות פגיעות, במיוחד קשישים ואנשים עם מוגבלויות. אחד האתגרים המרכזיים הוא מחסור בכוח אדם זמין שיכול לספק סיוע אישי ומהיר לאוכלוסיות אלו. לעיתים מספר המתנדבים או העובדים הסוציאליים אינו מספיק כדי לתת מענה לכל מי שזקוק לעזרה."
- "[מג'דל שמס] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=92% (100%, 75%, 100%) — נצפית שחיקה בקרב עובדי הרשות - נוכחות קרוב ל 50 אחוז .
הגישה של הרשות מחמירה לכן גם כשיש משאבים לא מפעילים אותם למשל הייתה המלצה לפתוח את מעדון הקשישים בתוך מרחב מוגן שקיים - הרשות התנגדה 
 מרכז חוסן פעיל מאד ומקיים פעילות הפגה באמצעות הזום"
- "[מגדל תפן] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=100% (100%, 100%, 100%) — לא רלוונטי"
- "[מזרעה] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=50% (50%, 50%, 50%) — ברשות ישנה קבוצת סער , בביום שישי בשבוע הבא מתוכנן תרגיל סער"
- "[מטה אשר] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=92% (100%, 100%, 75%) — ללא חריג"
- "[מסעדה] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=67% (75%, 50%, 75%) — אין פערים במיצוי משאבי קהילה"
- "[מעיליא] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=75% (75%, 75%, 75%) — קיים ביישוב תוכנית מתנדבים שמפעילה את הסער וקבוצת מתנדבים נוספים לפי מכלולים וכמו כן קיים כוח רפואי רופאים ואחיות בעת הצורך"
- "[משגב] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=92% (100%, 75%, 100%) — תחום ריכוז ההתנדבות אוייש על ידי אופיר אסולין (בחור מעצמון) אחרי תקופה מאד ארוכה שלא היה מאוייש. באופן כללי, יש התגייסות יפה של מתנדבים ביישובים. האתגר הוא לנהל את המשאב הזה נכון כדי למנוע שחיקה של האנשים לאורך זמן. בדגש עכשיו על כניסה לחופשת פסח ותגבור הפעילויות החינוכיות והבלתי פורמליות והתבססות הרבה מהפעמים על מתנדבים במקום או בנוסף לצוותים האורגניים."
- "[נהריה] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=75% (75%, 75%, 75%) — לנצל את בני הנוער הבוגרים אשר יכולים לסייע לרשות בהתנדבות"
- "[נחף] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=58% (50%, 75%, 50%) — הרשות עובדת כעת על גיוס מתנדבים נוספים שיעזרו בשעת הצורך בכל הנוגע להתנדבות במצב הביטחוני"
- "[סאג'ור] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=75% (75%, 75%, 75%) — תמיד יש חשש מריבוי התקהלות במידה ויהיה אירוע וזה מה שקרה שנפלו שברי יירוטים בכפר"
- "[סח'נין] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=58% (50%, 50%, 75%) — על אף הנכונות הרבה באוכלוסיה לתרום אין גוף המאגד ומנצל את המשאב האנושי 
יש יוזמות פה ושם אבל זה רחוק מלהיות מנוצל כראוי

קיים צורך משמעותי בהתנדבות בתחומים של סיוע לאוכלוסיות מוחלשות (קשישים, משפחות במצוקה), תמיכה רגשית-חברתית במיוחד בתקופות חירום, סיוע לוגיסטי וחלוקת משאבים, חינוך והכוונה לבני נוער, והנגשת מידע וזכויות לתושבים. בנוסף, יש צורך בחיזוק מערך ההתנדבות לשעת חירום והכשרת פעילים"
- "[ע'ג'ר] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=100% (100%, 100%, 100%) — קיימת יחידת ההתנדבות , היחידה מגויסת ומצוידת פועלת לפי קריאה , בשלב זה אין יוזמות והתארגנויות של תושבים"
- "[עילבון] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=75% (75%, 75%, 75%) — במועצה יש קבוצת מתנדבים ויש את המוכנות להיות בכל אירוע"
- "[עין קנייא] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=75% (75%, 75%, 75%) — משקיעים בקבוצות המתנדבים ובגיוס מתנדבים ליחידות התנדבות שונות כמו סע"ר וצח"ש"
- "[עכו] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=75% (75%, 75%, 75%) — ללא שינוי"
- "[עראבה] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=75% (75%, 75%, 75%) — התנדבות 
רכזת מתנדבים למא עאסלה הם צח״ש שחלקם קבוצת סע״ר . 
יש רשימות .
אתגרים ופערים , הכשרה פרקטית וציוד ."
- "[פסוטה] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=75% (75%, 75%, 75%) — קיימת ביישוב תכנית מתנדבי שמפעילה את הסער ומוקד 106 יישובי כמו כן קיים כח רפוא של רופאים ואחים ,אבל עדיין קיים פער בכמות המתנדבים המועטה"
- "[פקיעין] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=100% (100%, 100%, 100%) — רמת ההתנדבות הכפר גבוהה מאוד הן בשגרה והן בחירום"
- "[צפת] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=75% (75%, 75%, 75%) — ניתן לציין לחיוב כי קיימת נכונות גבוהה מצד תושבים לקחת חלק בפעילות התנדבותית, המעידה על תחושת אחריות קהילתית וסולידריות עמוקה. תושבים רבים מביעים רצון לתרום, לסייע לאוכלוסיות פגיעות ולתמוך בפעילות העירונית. לדוגמא- טיפול בתקלות מקלוט, סיוע להורים שנמצאים עם ילדיהם בבית(השירות הפסיכולוגי) הפעלות בזום לילדים ועוד. פעילויות וחלוקת ערכות הפגה לילדים במקלטים. התנדבות בתחומי לימודים והעשרה. גרעין מתנדבים שמגיע לעזור לנשות המילואמניקים בביתם בניקיון ארוחות בייביסיטר תיקונים קטנים בבית וחבילות שי. כמו כן, נפתחה קבוצת וואצפ ייעודית לנשות המילואים על מנת להקל על העומס."
- "[קצרין] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=83% (75%, 100%, 75%) — אין אתגרים או פערים שידועים לי, יחד עם זאת המתנדבים פועלים במספר ערוצים-
1. עזרה למשפחות המילואים- ביביסיטר, ניקיון, בישול
2. ילדי צמי"ד- פעילויות הפגה בכל יום
3. חלוקת מזון לנזקקים
4.צח"שים- מגיעים לבתי תושבים בגיל השלישי להפגת בדידות
5. הכנת מארזים לוותיקים וחלוקה"
- "[ראמה] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=100% (100%, 100%, 100%) — לרשות עומדים מספר מקורות מתנדבים, הראשון זה יחידת סע"ר הכוללת כ- 50 מתנדבים פעילים ומתורגלים היטב, נוסף על זאת קיים מערך מתנדבים ש לבעלי מקצועות חופשיים ובעלי הכשרות רפואיות כמו חובשים, וכו.. מערך הרווחה קיים וערוך לכל פניה, עד כה לא נצפו אתגרים או פערים במיצוי המשאבים"
- "[שלומי] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=67% (75%, 75%, 50%) — אני רואה שהמועצה מאוד מחוייבת להפעלה של מוקדי חינוך ונעזרת בארגון אחד הקיים אולם אין מנגנון להפעלה של מתנדבים מתוך העיר בנושאי קהילה. אנחנו מרכזים מאמץ בסס שני עם המלשכיות על מנת לךהסביר לתושבים שאין להם מיגון זמין איפה המקום הכי בטוח להם בבית"
- "[שעב] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=75% (75%, 75%, 75%) — אין מספיק מקלטים ומקומות לקיום פעילות הסברה גם לעמותות וגם אין מספיק ציוד אישי למתנדבים"
- "[שפרעם] הון ומשאבי קהילה: avg=58% (75%, 25%, 75%) — הצופים הנוצרים מסתובבים בעיר ופועלים בקרה הקהילה הנוצרית. אין רכז מתנדבים. למעשה כל קהילה למען עצמה"
